- **adr-conformance-reviewer**: Reviews a code diff for conformance to the project's immutable ADRs. Give it the ADR file path(s); it checks layout, frontmatter, and writing-rule violations without any conversation context. ([key/key-plugin-cc](https://github.com/key/key-plugin-cc/blob/HEAD/plugins/madr-toolkit/agents/adr-reviewer.md) · ★1)
- **adr-reviewer**: Read-only reviewer for ADR files. Validates each ADR against the spark ADR template by running deterministic structural checks (D01–D11) per file, then reports findings by severity. Does not modify or fix files — output only. Receives resolved folder paths from the Spark orchestrator. Use whenever a user asks to "review the ADRs", "check an ADR", "validate ADR-0001", or "find issues in the decision records". ([busadave13/spark](https://github.com/busadave13/spark/blob/HEAD/plugins/spark/agents/adr-reviewer.agent.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Reviews a plan, design doc, spec change or git diff against the repository's locked ADRs (Architecture Decision Records). Reports specific violations with an ADR + line reference. Use whenever a planned change touches architecture, conventions, infrastructure or process — e.g. "adr review", "check the ADRs", "review against the decisions", "does this break any ADR?", or before merging a change that affects the stack, patterns or repo layout. ([chacal88/aidakit](https://github.com/chacal88/aidakit/blob/HEAD/agents/adr-reviewer.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Reviews a plan, design doc, spec change or diff against the project's locked ADRs (Architecture Decision Records) and returns one verdict with each violation tied to an ADR section and a file:line. Use when a change touches architecture, stack, conventions, infrastructure or process, or someone asks "adr review", "check the ADRs", "does this break any decision?". ([chacal88/devkit](https://github.com/chacal88/devkit/blob/HEAD/plugins/core/agents/adr-reviewer.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Reviews Architecture Decision Record documents themselves, one draft or a whole ADR directory, against the software-architecture:architecture-decision-records practice. Checks the scope test, the feature smell (an ADR that reads like a feature spec), the six-section anatomy, lifecycle and link hygiene, remember and revolt against the Context Sources in ARCHITECTURE.md, and radar evidence for technology adoption. Returns findings with evidence and a proposed fix. Read-only. Use before an ADR is committed or accepted, from /software-architecture:adr-audit, and when architecture-reviewer finds A… ([Integral-Productivity/software-architecture-claude-plugin](https://github.com/Integral-Productivity/software-architecture-claude-plugin/blob/HEAD/agents/adr-reviewer.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Use this agent to adversarially review a proposed ADR for conflicts with the immutable engineering baseline and existing accepted decisions. Returns approve or veto. Trigger when the kernel's cf_next_step returns an adr_review step. ([jwilger/claude-factory](https://github.com/jwilger/claude-factory/blob/HEAD/plugins/claude-factory/agents/adr-reviewer.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Reviews a unit's diff against the ADRs it names and the repository boundaries. Use in /build state 6 on every unit; it reports every deviation with the ADR id and file:line so the builder can fix or answer each one. ([kapdroid/Fa-Lens](https://github.com/kapdroid/Fa-Lens/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/adr-reviewer.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Read-only critic for MADR 4.0 ADRs. Invoke after drafting `docs/decisions/NNNN-*.md` to check section completeness, whether the options were real, honest consequences, and conflicts with accepted ADRs and project rules. Does NOT write files. ([Lenivvenil/mach](https://github.com/Lenivvenil/mach/blob/HEAD/plugin/agents/adr-reviewer.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Reviews a diff, plan, or file set against the ADRs in docs/adr and reports violations with the ADR number, file:line, and the smallest fix. Use before PRs touching apps/api/src/core, auth, db schema, or shared packages, and whenever asked "does this follow our architecture?". Read-only. ([machxi-dev/machxi-starter](https://github.com/machxi-dev/machxi-starter/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/adr-reviewer.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Stand-in for the ADR reviewer in the failed-check evals. Always reports one failure the drafting agent can correct, so every run reaches the correction check. ([michaelmarconi/agentic-engineering](https://github.com/michaelmarconi/agentic-engineering/blob/HEAD/evals-failed-check/agents/adr-reviewer.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Reviews one ADR (architecture decision record) against the ADR rubric and reports pass or fail for each check, with evidence. Started by the adr skill with `Document: &lt;path&gt;` as its request's first line. To review an ADR, use the adr skill rather than starting this agent yourself: the skill runs the whole review sequence. ([michaelmarconi/agentic-engineering](https://github.com/michaelmarconi/agentic-engineering/blob/HEAD/agents/adr-reviewer.md))
- **adr-reviewer**: Reviews a completed implementation against the Accepted ADRs and approved plan that specified it, then fixes what it finds. Use in the review phase of the adr-rpi workflow, after adr-implementer returns. Checks correctness, scope fidelity, test coverage, observability, and repo-convention drift; re-runs verification after each fix. Escalates instead of fixing when the only resolution would contradict an accepted decision. ([shr3kst3r/dev-tools](https://github.com/shr3kst3r/dev-tools/blob/HEAD/agents/adr-reviewer.md))

- **adr-scorer**: Reviews one draft ADR — routes it (is it an ADR, or a rule, value, story, reference, idea, open item or nothing) and scores its alternatives 0–3, listing the cheap options not weighed. Task, guide excerpt, record inventory, primitives and the ADR all come pasted in the prompt; the agent reads nothing beyond its prompt and its rendered body. ([A/claude-booping](https://github.com/A/claude-booping/blob/HEAD/agents/adr-scorer.md) · ★15)
- **adr-scout**: Surfaces prior ADRs, shipped seams, and live estate/code that a proposed decision must reconcile with — BEFORE a new ADR is drafted. Use at the START of any non-trivial design, and whenever a feature feels "greenfield." Prevents the failure where a new ADR duplicates, couples to, or silently contradicts an already-accepted decision (e.g. designing a provisioning reach-path while ADR-0058 already ships one). Reports the related decisions, what each already ships, and the reconciliation the new work owes each. ([dstout-devops/stratt](https://github.com/dstout-devops/stratt/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/adr-scout.md))

([neonwatty/qa-skills](https://github.com/neonwatty/qa-skills/blob/HEAD/agents/adversarial-breaker.md) · ★34 · 2 copies)
- **adversarial-challenger**: Read-only adversarial challenger. Stress-tests any claim, plan, design, or decision. - **adversarial-execution-reviewer**: Use before shipping any change to the execution surface — worker dispatch, operation gates, plan acceptance, morphism application, evidence attachment, or the case-space store. Attacks the trust model instead of reviewing the code, and checks every claim in the security policy against what the code enforces. ([CAPHTECH/casegraphen](https://github.com/CAPHTECH/casegraphen/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/adversarial-execution-reviewer.md))
- **adversarial-expert**: Use this agent when you need a brutally honest, adversarial assessment of code quality, security, or architectural soundness. This agent assumes the persona of a real, skeptical, expert who asks, 'why should I trust 100k+ lines of vibe-code, created by someone who doesn't even know half the languages used?' -- and is then paid handsomely to review it themself. This agent assumes nothing works, and everything is compromised, until proven otherwise. It reads code line-by-line and asks: would I stake my professional reputation on this? Shuold anybody care about or trust it, much less use it? ([limn-works/scp](https://github.com/limn-works/scp/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/adversarial-expert.md) · ★1)
- **adversarial-final-reviewer**: Actively try to prove GhostInit v0.1 is incomplete. Search for placeholders, skipped tests, hidden assumptions, security leaks, and release gaps. ([aymenmerabta5/ghostinit](https://github.com/aymenmerabta5/ghostinit/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/adversarial-final-reviewer.md))
- **adversarial-general**: Holistic "what's missing" review: hunts for completeness gaps, missing defenses, operational blindness, and documentation debt that specialist reviewers are explicitly scoped not to cover. - **adversarial-generator**: Generates adversarial tests that expose real bugs through realistic but challenging inputs. Use when creating tests designed to find logic errors. - **adversarial-implementer**: Adversarial Critic for the implementation domain of the verification layer. - **adversarial-input-reviewer**: Reviews a diff for missing input validation, resource amplification, and panics on untrusted input (INV-2, INV-4, INV-5 in docs/ai/invariants.md). - **adversarial-integrator**: Read-only integrator that deduplicates adversarial findings, verifies evidence, and normalizes severity without inventing issues. Launched by the /review skill. - **adversarial-review**: Finds concrete defect, regression, security, and compatibility risks without an approval verdict. ([buYoung/agents](https://github.com/buYoung/agents/blob/HEAD/packages/claude-code/agents/adversarial-review.md) · ★1)
- **adversarial-review**: Read-only adversarial teardown of a change, plan, spec, or design. Hunts for the weakest point through escalating passes and returns a Kill Report of substantiated failures. Use for non-trivial, risky, or user-facing work. Never edits files. ([dvnc0/dev-conductor](https://github.com/dvnc0/dev-conductor/blob/HEAD/agents/adversarial-review.md) · ★1)
- **adversarial-review**: Multi-model adversarial code review with 4-way voting reconciliation. Supports review-only and review-and-fix modes with cross-session dismissed-findings persistence. Invoke explicitly only. ([KevinBrown5280/fun-with-copilot](https://github.com/KevinBrown5280/fun-with-copilot/blob/HEAD/plugins/adversarial-review/agents/adversarial-review.agent.md) · ★1)
- **adversarial-review**: Adversarial code review — assumes the implementation is wrong and hunts for ways it fails. Read-only, reports findings without making changes, high reasoning effort. ([repro-dev/repro](https://github.com/repro-dev/repro/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/adversarial-review.md) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **adversarial-review**: 敵対的レビュー用。ステージ済み・未ステージ・未追跡ファイルを含む作業ツリーの変更をレビューするときに使う。正確性・回帰・要件漏れを疑い、根拠のある不具合だけ報告する。 ([Widthdom/GHCAgentRefinement](https://github.com/Widthdom/GHCAgentRefinement/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/adversarial-review.agent.md) · ★1)
- **adversarial-review**: Reviews a finished change against the Contract, looking for ways it breaks. Fresh eyes - give it only a commit range (or "the working tree"), never the author's reasoning or what you think is wrong. Use after a story's core and web are committed, before a fix is committed, or whenever the user asks for a review. Reports findings; changes nothing. ([kunlabora/gorefest](https://github.com/kunlabora/gorefest/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/adversarial-review.md))
- **adversarial-review**: Critically reviews a reference document with a devil's advocate perspective, grounded in codebase evidence. ([miketdonahue/my-skills](https://github.com/miketdonahue/my-skills/blob/HEAD/agents/adversarial-review.md))
- **adversarial-review**: **Principle:** A second agent is pointed at the work with one job — **prove it's wrong, unsafe, or incomplete**. Not "review this", not "any thoughts?". Its success condition is finding a defect, and its verdict is a gate that can stop a merge. ([trurner13/house-rules](https://github.com/trurner13/house-rules/blob/HEAD/knowledge/agents/adversarial-review.md))
- **adversarial-review**: Attacks a finding, design, plan, patch or conclusion and tries to break it, reporting only failures it can actually demonstrate. - **adversarial-review-agent**: Argues that a diff should not merge. Runs five frames against an anchored goal and returns BLOCK, REWORK or CONCEDE. - **adversarial-reviewer**: Attacks a conclusion, result, design or synthesis — finds the hidden assumption, the failure case, the reason it is wrong. - **adversarial-reviewer**: General-purpose adversarial reviewer for CAMARF findings, code changes, or claims — instructed to argue AGAINST the claim and try to break it, not confirm it. - **adversarial-reviewer**: Round-1 correctness reviewer with a hostile mandate. Use PROACTIVELY before implementing a spec/plan and again on the whole branch before a PR. Never the author. Read-only — verdict first, then numbered P0/P1/P2 findings with file:line and a concrete fix. (Generic user-level version; a repo's .claude/agents/ copy overrides it.) ([SloopWorks/dayfold](https://github.com/SloopWorks/dayfold/blob/HEAD/processes/claude-home/agents/adversarial-reviewer.md))
- **adversarial-reviewer**: Single-pass skeptical review — assumes the code is wrong until proven otherwise, challenges assumptions and architectural decisions. ([smartwatermelon/smartwatermelon-marketplace](https://github.com/smartwatermelon/smartwatermelon-marketplace/blob/HEAD/plugins/code-critic/agents/adversarial-reviewer.md))
- **adversarial-reviewer**: Adversarial reviewer dispatched by the adversarial-review skill. Expects a prepared task with mode, exact slice, collection guidance, intent, and optional focus. ([davcs86/agent-plugins](https://github.com/davcs86/agent-plugins/blob/HEAD/plugins/design-buddy/agents/adversary.md) · ★1)
- **adversary**: The attack pass on a RISKY diff: one that touches authentication, sessions, access rules, input parsing or privacy filters (`risky_diff.py` says so). Tries to break the change with hostile input and state, runs what it can, and returns a `VERDICT: CLEAN` or `VERDICT: BLOCKED` with the input that got through. Runs on Fable, the owner's model for attack passes. Read-only: it never edits the repository, and the caller saves its answer as the record. ([fmanimashaun/claude-skills](https://github.com/fmanimashaun/claude-skills/blob/HEAD/plugins/rails-flow/agents/adversary.md) · ★1)
- **adversary**: Adversarial design reviewer. Reviews a plan or a changeset at architecture altitude — structural soundness, maintainability, alternatives — and is deliberately skeptical to counter an author's self-approval bias. Explicitly does NOT do style, formatting, nits, or minor/local bugs (linters, type-checkers, /code-review, and caveman:cavecrew-reviewer own those). Read-only: it never modifies code. Spawned by /jador:critique. ([Jador/skills](https://github.com/Jador/skills/blob/HEAD/agents/adversary.md) · ★1)
- **adversary**: The third and final v1 agent in `spp`, and the only one that is **opt-in**. The adversary is enabled by setting `ADVERSARY_FLAG = on` in `plan.md` §8 and the corresponding field in `loop_spec.md` §4; off by default. ([JayLBean/supervised-prompt-producer](https://github.com/JayLBean/supervised-prompt-producer/blob/HEAD/skills/run/agents/adversary.md) · ★1)
- **adversary**: FRONTIER-DO tier at xhigh effort. Adversarial correctness reviewer for high-stakes or correctness-critical diffs. Read-only. Tries to BREAK the change - find fail-open paths, bypasses, false-positive/negative gaps, tamper vectors, and defect classes that survive the tests. Never edits; returns severity-ranked findings. Use before merging logic where a silent wrong answer is expensive (gates, validators, money/identity/security paths, normalize/parse code). ([LCS-Adam/standing-orders](https://github.com/LCS-Adam/standing-orders/blob/HEAD/agents/adversary.md) · ★1)
- **adversary**: The Auditor role persona of the adversary-pipeline skill — hyper-skeptical, factual, and strictly avoids hallucination or flattery. Synthesizes Attacker/Defender findings into a prioritized risk report; not a standalone single-pass reviewer. ([mapengbo0314/native-harness-workflow](https://github.com/mapengbo0314/native-harness-workflow/blob/HEAD/src/harness/templates/boilerplate/agents/adversary.md) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **adversary**: An adversarial agent that is hyper-skeptical, factual, and strictly avoids hallucination or flattery. ([mapengbo0314/native-harness-workflow](https://github.com/mapengbo0314/native-harness-workflow/blob/HEAD/.gemini/agents/adversary.md) · ★1)
- **Adversary**: Tries to break a change and proves each finding with a run. Used by the interrogate skill, usually several at once on different model families. ([mattcosta7/dotfiles](https://github.com/mattcosta7/dotfiles/blob/HEAD/copilot/agents/adversary.agent.md) · ★1)
- **adversary**: Adversarial verifier — given a single claim, finding, conclusion, or proposed fix, its job is to REFUTE it, not confirm it. Verifies independently (reads the code, runs the check, constructs counterexamples) and never trusts the claim's own reasoning. Returns a structured verdict: refuted (with concrete evidence) / survives (tried hard, couldn't break it) / uncertain. Biases toward "refuted" when genuinely uncertain (a false refutation is cheap to dismiss; a missed flaw is expensive). The caller supplies the exact claim and any context in the dispatch prompt. ([Nitjsefnie/burn-usage-skill](https://github.com/Nitjsefnie/burn-usage-skill/blob/HEAD/agents/adversary.md) · ★1)
- **adversary**: Invoke once per candidate after scanner and, for T7 or T10, boundary tracing. Try to refute reachability from current code and return `candidate-assessment@1`. ([orin-dx/wisp-plugins](https://github.com/orin-dx/wisp-plugins/blob/HEAD/plugins/ranger/agents/adversary.md) · ★1)
- **adversary**: Adversary eye — enters VIRGIN and finds failure modes that make the implementation unviable. Read-only. ([orobsonn/claude-harness](https://github.com/orobsonn/claude-harness/blob/HEAD/core/opencode/agents/adversary.md) · ★1)
- **adversary**: Attack agent — enters virgin (no prior verdicts) and finds the failure modes that make the implementation unviable, ranked by irreversibility. Read-only. - **adversary**: Generates 2-3 additional debugging hypotheses that attack gaps in primary list — especially config, env, deployment, human error, upstream/downstream, invalid-premise failures.
